## Specification (func.py as given)
```python
def sum_of_scores(n: int, m: int) -> int:
    """ Calculate the sum of scores of all good sequences modulo 998244353.
    
    A good sequence is an integer sequence of length between 1 and n (inclusive),
    where each element is between 1 and m (inclusive).
    
    The score of a good sequence is the number of positive divisors of X,
    where X is the product of all elements in the sequence.
    
    The function computes the sum of scores of all possible good sequences.
    There are sum(m^k for k in range(1, n+1)) such sequences in total.
    
    Args:
        n: Maximum length of sequences (1 <= n <= 10^18)
        m: Maximum value of elements (1 <= m <= 16)
    
    Returns:
        The sum of all scores modulo 998244353
    
    >>> sum_of_scores(1, 7)
    16
    >>> sum_of_scores(3, 11)
    16095
    >>> sum_of_scores(81131, 14)
    182955659
    """
```

## Final func.py
```python
MOD = 998244353


def _mod_inv(a: int) -> int:
    """Return the modular inverse of a modulo MOD."""
    return pow(a, MOD - 2, MOD)


# Precompute binomial coefficients up to 7 (for recurrences).
_BINOM = [[0] * 8 for _ in range(8)]
for _i in range(8):
    _BINOM[_i][0] = _BINOM[_i][_i] = 1
    for _j in range(1, _i):
        _BINOM[_i][_j] = (_BINOM[_i - 1][_j - 1] + _BINOM[_i - 1][_j]) % MOD


def _compute_primes_W(m: int):
    """Return (primes, W) where primes is the list of primes <= m,
    and W[mask] = sum_{v=1..m} prod_{p in mask} e_p(v)  (mod MOD)."""
    primes = [p for p in (2, 3, 5, 7, 11, 13) if p <= m]
    P = len(primes)

    # Exponent vector for each value 1..m
    elem_exp = []
    for v in range(1, m + 1):
        vec = []
        x = v
        for p in primes:
            e = 0
            while x % p == 0:
                x //= p
                e += 1
            vec.append(e)
        elem_exp.append(tuple(vec))

    W = {}
    for mask in range(1 << P):
        total = 0
        for vec in elem_exp:
            prod = 1
            for i in range(P):
                if mask >> i & 1:
                    prod *= vec[i]
            total += prod
        W[mask] = total % MOD
    return primes, W


def _compute_A(primes, W):
    """Return A[t] = sum over all set partitions of any subset of primes
    into exactly t blocks of the product of W(block)."""
    P = len(primes)
    dp = [[0] * (P + 1) for _ in range(1 << P)]
    dp[0][0] = 1  # empty set, 0 blocks

    for mask in range(1, 1 << P):
        lsb = mask & -mask
        first = (lsb.bit_length() - 1)
        rest = mask ^ lsb
        sub = rest
        while True:
            T = sub | lsb
            W_T = W[T]
            rem = mask ^ T
            for t in range(1, P + 1):
                if dp[rem][t - 1]:
                    dp[mask][t] = (dp[mask][t] + W_T * dp[rem][t - 1]) % MOD
            if sub == 0:
                break
            sub = (sub - 1) & rest

    A = [0] * (P + 1)
    for mask in range(1 << P):
        for t in range(P + 1):
            A[t] = (A[t] + dp[mask][t]) % MOD
    return A


def _compute_F(N: int, m: int, max_t: int):
    """Return F[t] = sum_{k=0}^{N} m^k * (k)_t  for t = 0..max_t (mod MOD)."""
    F = [0] * (max_t + 1)
    m_pow_N1 = pow(m, N + 1, MOD)
    inv_m1 = _mod_inv(m - 1) if m != 1 else None

    # (N+1)_t for t = 0..max_t+1
    n1 = (N + 1) % MOD
    fall_n1 = [1] * (max_t + 2)
    for t in range(1, max_t + 2):
        fall_n1[t] = fall_n1[t - 1] * ((n1 - t + 1) % MOD) % MOD

    if m == 1:
        # F_t(N) = (N+1)_{t+1} / (t+1)
        for t in range(max_t + 1):
            F[t] = fall_n1[t + 1] * _mod_inv(t + 1) % MOD
    else:
        F[0] = (m_pow_N1 - 1) * inv_m1 % MOD
        for t in range(1, max_t + 1):
            term = m_pow_N1 * fall_n1[t] % MOD
            term = (term - m * t % MOD * F[t - 1]) % MOD
            F[t] = term * inv_m1 % MOD
    return F


def sum_of_scores(n: int, m: int) -> int:
    """ Calculate the sum of scores of all good sequences modulo 998244353.
    
    A good sequence is an integer sequence of length between 1 and n (inclusive),
    where each element is between 1 and m (inclusive).
    
    The score of a good sequence is the number of positive divisors of X,
    where X is the product of all elements in the sequence.
    
    The function computes the sum of scores of all possible good sequences.
    There are sum(m^k for k in range(1, n+1)) such sequences in total.
    
    Args:
        n: Maximum length of sequences (1 <= n <= 10^18)
        m: Maximum value of elements (1 <= m <= 16)
    
    Returns:
        The sum of all scores modulo 998244353
    
    >>> sum_of_scores(1, 7)
    16
    >>> sum_of_scores(3, 11)
    16095
    >>> sum_of_scores(81131, 14)
    182955659
    """
    if m == 1:
        return n % MOD

    primes, W = _compute_primes_W(m)
    P = len(primes)
    A = _compute_A(primes, W)

    # F[t] = sum_{k=0}^n m^k * (k)_t
    F = _compute_F(n, m, P)

    inv_m = _mod_inv(m)

    # total = A_0 * (F[0] - 1) + sum_{t=1}^P A_t * m^{-t} * F[t]
    total = A[0] * (F[0] - 1) % MOD
    inv_m_pow = 1  # m^0
    for t in range(1, P + 1):
        inv_m_pow = inv_m_pow * inv_m % MOD
        total = (total + A[t] * inv_m_pow % MOD * F[t]) % MOD

    return total
```

{
 "code": "# Let's implement the solution step by step.\n\nMOD = 998244353\n\ndef compute_W_and_A(m):\n    \"\"\"Compute W(T) for all T subset of primes, and A_t for t=0..|P|\"\"\"\n    primes = []\n    for p in [2, 3,5,7,11,13]:\n        if p <= m:\n            primes.append(p)\n    \n    # Actually, primes should be those that divide some number <= m\n    # But for m <= 16, the only primes dividing numbers <= m are those <= m\n    # Let's just take all primes <= m\n    primes = [p for p in [2,3,5,7,11,13] if p <= m]\n    P = len(primes)\n    \n    # Precompute exponent of each prime in each value 1..m\n    elem_exp = []\n    for v in range(1, m+1):\n        vec = []\n        x = v\n        for p in primes:\n            e = 0\n            while x % p == 0:\n                x //= p\n                e += 1\n            vec.append(e)\n        elem_exp.append(vec)\n    \n    # W(T) = sum_{v=1}^m prod_{p in T} e_p(v)\n    # We'll compute for all subsets\n    W = {}\n    for mask in range(1 << P):\n        total = 0\n        for v_idx, vec in enumerate(elem_exp):\n            prod = 1\n            for i, p in enumerate(primes):\n                if mask >> i & 1:\n                    prod *= vec[i]\n            total += prod\n        W[mask] = total % MOD\n    return primes, W\n\n# Test\nprimes, W = compute_W_and_A(2)\nprint(\"m=2, primes:\", primes)\nfor mask, val in sorted(W.items()):\n    print(f\"  mask {mask:01b} (len={bin(mask).count('1')}): {val}\")\n\nprimes, W = compute_W_and_A(3)\nprint(\"\\nm=3, primes:\", primes)\nfor mask, val in sorted(W.items()):\n    print(f\"  mask {mask:02b}: {val}\")"

A_0 = 1, A_1 = 2, A_2 = 1

S_k = 3^k * 1 + 3^{k-1} * k * 2 + 3^{k-2} * k(k-1) * 1

Let me verify with the brute force:
k=1: 3 + 1*2 + 0 = 5 ✓
k=2: 9 + 3*2*2 + 1*2*1 = 9 + 12 + 2 = 23 ✓
k=3: 27 + 9*3*2 + 3*3*2*1 = 27 + 54 + 18 = 99 ✓
k=4: 81 + 27*4*2 + 9*4*3*1 = 81 + 216 + 108 = 405 ✓

Great!

Now let's compute for m=4:
m=4, primes are still {2, 3} (since 4 = 2^2).
W values:
- W(∅) = 4
- W({2}) = e_2(1)+e_2(2)+e_2(3)+e_2(4) = 0+1+0+2 = 3
- W({3}) = e_3(1)+e_3(2)+e_3(3)+e_3(4) = 0+0+1+0 = 1
- W({2,3}) = e_2*e_3 sum = 0*0 + 1*0 + 0*1 + 2*0 = 0

A_0 = 1 (empty partition of empty set)
A_1 = W({2}) + W({3}) = 3 + 1 = 4 (partitions of a 1-element set: {2} and {3})
A_2 = W({2,3}) + W({2})*W({3}) = 0 + 3*1 = 3 (partition of {2,3} into one block, or two blocks)

So A = [1, 4, 3]. Matches!

Now let me verify S_k formula for m=4:
k=1: 4^1*1 + 4^0*1*4 + 4^{-1}*0*3 = 4 + 4 + 0 = 8 ✓ (from brute: 8)
k=2: 4^2 + 4^1*2*4 + 4^0*2*1*3 = 16 + 32 + 6 = 54 ✓
k=3: 4^3 + 4^2*3*4 + 4^1*3*2*3 = 64 + 192 + 72 = 328 ✓

Now the formula is confirmed. 

Now I need to compute:
Total = ∑_{k=1}^n S_k = ∑_{k=1}^n ∑_{t=0}^P m^{k-t} * (k)_t * A_t

= ∑_{t=0}^P A_t * m^{-t} * ∑_{k=1}^n m^k * (k)_t

where (k)_t = k * (k-1) * ... * (k-t+1) with (k)_0 = 1.

Let me define:
F_t(n) = ∑_{k=1}^n m^k * (k)_t

We need to compute F_t(n) for t = 0..P.

Now, (k)_t is a polynomial in k of degree t. We can write:

(k)_t = ∑_{j=0}^t s(t, j) * k^j

where s(t, j) are Stirling numbers of the first kind (signed). Actually, the unsigned Stirling numbers of the first kind: (k)_t = ∑_{j=0}^t (-1)^{t-j} * c(t, j) * k^j.

Or we can work directly with falling factorials.

Actually, there's a nice formula for ∑ m^k * (k)_t:

Consider the generating function:
G(x) = ∑_{k=0}^n m^k * x^k = (m^{n+1} x^{n+1} - 1) / (mx - 1)

But we need (k)_t. Note that (k)_t = [d^t/dx^t x^k] evaluated at x=1? No.

Actually, (k)_t * m^k = m^k * k! / (k-t)! 

Let me compute ∑_{k=t}^n m^k * k! / (k-t)! 

Let j = k-t:
= ∑_{j=0}^{n-t} m^{j+t} * (j+t)! / j!
= m^t * ∑_{j=0}^{n-t} m^j * (j+t)! / j!

Now, (j+t)! / j! = (j+t)(j+t-1)...(j+1) = ∏_{i=1}^t (j+i)

This is a polynomial in j of degree t.

So we can expand:
(j+t)! / j! = ∑_{i=0}^t a_{t,i} * j^i

And then:
F_t(n) = m^t * ∑_{i=0}^t a_{t,i} * ∑_{j=0}^{n-t} m^j * j^i

So we need to compute sums of the form:
G_i(N) = ∑_{j=0}^N m^j * j^i

for i = 0..P and N = n - t.

We can compute G_i(N) using a recurrence or using the formula for geometric sums with polynomial coefficients.

Let me derive a method to compute G_i(N) efficiently.

We can use the following recurrence:

For m = 1:
G_i(N) = ∑_{j=0}^N j^i, which are the Faulhaber sums. We can compute using Bernoulli numbers or formulas for small i.

For m ≠ 1:
We can use the following trick:

Let H_i(N) = ∑_{j=0}^N m^j * j^i

Consider:
(H_i(N) - m * H_i(N)) = ∑_{j=0}^N m^j * j^i - ∑_{j=0}^N m^{j+1} * j^i
= ∑_{j=0}^N m^j * j^i - ∑_{j=1}^{N+1} m^j * (j-1)^i
= 0^i + ∑_{j=1}^N m^j * (j^i - (j-1)^i) - m^{N+1} * N^i

Now, j^i - (j-1)^i = ∑_{r=0}^{i-1} C(i, r) * j^r * (-1)^{i-1-r}? Actually:

(j-1)^i = ∑_{r=0}^i C(i, r) * j^r * (-1)^{i-r}
So j^i - (j-1)^i = j^i - ∑_{r=0}^i C(i, r) j^r (-1)^{i-r}
= j^i - [j^i * (-1)^0 + ∑_{r=0}^{i-1} C(i, r) j^r (-1)^{i-r}]
= j^i - j^i - ∑_{r=0}^{i-1} C(i, r) j^r (-1)^{i-r}
= - ∑_{r=0}^{i-1} C(i, r) j^r (-1)^{i-r}
= ∑_{r=0}^{i-1} C(i, r) j^r (-1)^{i-r+1}

So:
(1-m) * H_i(N) = 0^i + ∑_{r=0}^{i-1} C(i, r) (-1)^{i-r+1} * H_r(N) - m^{N+1} * N^i

Where 0^i is 1 if i=0 else 0.

Thus:
H_i(N) = (m^{N+1} * N^i - 0^i - ∑_{r=0}^{i-1} C(i, r) (-1)^{i-r+1} * H_r(N)) * inv(m-1)

This gives H_i(N) in terms of H_{<i}(N). We can compute all H_i for i=0..P in O(P^2) time.

But wait, N can be up to n = 10^18, so we can't iterate. We just need the closed form.

From the recurrence, we can compute H_i(N) for any N using O(P^2) operations, given m^N mod MOD.

Let me verify this recurrence.

Actually, let's first handle the case m = 1 separately.

For m = 1:
H_i(N) = ∑_{j=0}^N j^i

We can compute this using Bernoulli numbers or using the formula:
∑_{j=0}^N j^i = 1/(i+1) * ∑_{r=0}^i C(i+1, r) * B_r * N^{i+1-r}

where B_r are Bernoulli numbers. Since i ≤ 6, we can just hardcode formulas.

Alternatively, we can use the recurrence:
(N+1)^{i+1} - 0^{i+1} = ∑_{j=0}^N ((j+1)^{i+1} - j^{i+1})
= ∑_{j=0}^N ∑_{r=0}^i C(i+1, r) j^r
= ∑_{r=0}^i C(i+1, r) H_r(N)

So:
(N+1)^{i+1} = ∑_{r=0}^i C(i+1, r) H_r(N) + H_i(N) * C(i+1, i)
= ∑_{r=0}^{i-1} C(i+1, r) H_r(N) + H_i(N) * (i+1)

Thus:
H_i(N) = ((N+1)^{i+1} - ∑_{r=0}^{i-1} C(i+1, r) H_r(N)) / (i+1)

This gives a recurrence for the m=1 case.

OK, so now the plan:

1. For given m, find primes ≤ m.
2. Compute W(T) for all subsets T of primes.
3. Compute A_t for t = 0..|P|.
4. Compute the coefficients c_{t,j} such that (k)_t = ∑_{j=0}^t s_{t,j} * k^j (Stirling numbers of the first kind, unsigned).
   Actually, (k)_t = ∑_{j=0}^t (-1)^{t-j} * c(t,j) * k^j where c(t,j) are unsigned Stirling numbers of first kind.
   Or better: let's just precompute the conversion from falling factorial basis to power basis.

5. Then S_k = ∑_{t=0}^P A_t * m^{k-t} * ∑_{j=0}^t s_{t,j} * k^j
   = m^k * ∑_{j=0}^P (∑_{t=j}^P A_t * m^{-t} * s_{t,j}) * k^j

Let C_j = ∑_{t=j}^P A_t * m^{-t} * s_{t,j} mod MOD.

6. Total = ∑_{k=1}^n S_k = ∑_{j=0}^P C_j * ∑_{k=1}^n m^k * k^j

So we need H_j(n) = ∑_{k=1}^n m^k * k^j (with H_j(0) = 0).

Actually, H_j(n) = G_j(n) where G_j(N) = ∑_{k=0}^N m^k * k^j, and H_j(n) = G_j(n) - G_j(0) = G_j(n) - 0^j.

For m=1: G_j(n) = ∑_{k=0}^n k^j. H_j(n) = G_j(n) - 0^j.

Let me implement this step by step.

 "code": "MOD = 998244353\n\n# Helper functions\ndef mod_pow(a, e):\n    return pow(a, e, MOD)\n\ndef mod_inv(a):\n    return pow(a, MOD-2, MOD)\n\n# Stirling numbers of the first kind (unsigned)\n# c[n][k] = number of permutations of n elements with k cycles\n# (x)_n = sum_{k=0}^n (-1)^{n-k} * c[n][k] * x^k\n# Also: (x)_n = sum_{k=0}^n s1[n][k] * x^k where s1 are signed Stirling numbers of first kind\n# s1[n][k] = (-1)^{n-k} * c[n][k]\n\n# Let's compute signed Stirling numbers s1[n][k]\ndef stirling1_signed(n, k):\n    # recurrence: s1[n+1][k] = s1[n][k-1] - n * s1[n][k]\n    s1 = [[0]*(n+1) for _ in range(n+1)]\n    s1[0][0] = 1\n    for i in range(1, n+1):\n        for j in range(1, i+1):\n            s1[i][j] = (s1[i-1][j-1] - (i-1) * s1[i-1][j]) % MOD\n    return s1[n][k]\n\n# Precompute all s1 up to 6\ns1 = [[0]*7 for _ in range(7)]\ns1[0][0] = 1\nfor i in range(1, 7):\n    for j in range(1, i+1):\n        s1[i][j] = (s1[i-1][j-1] - (i-1) * s1[i-1][j]) % MOD\n\nprint(\"Signed Stirling numbers of first kind s1[n][k]:\")\nfor i in range(7):\n    print(f\"n={i}: {[s1[i][j] for j in range(i+1)]}\")\n\n# Let's verify: (k)_t = sum_j s1[t][j] * k^j\nfor t in range(7):\n    for k in range(7):\n        lhs = 1\n        for i in range(t):\n            lhs *= (k - i)\n        rhs = sum(s1[t][j] * (k**j) for j in range(t+1))\n        if lhs != rhs:\n            print(f\"Mismatch: t={t}, k={k}, lhs={lhs}, rhs={rhs}\")\nprint(\"Verification done\")"
}

- For m = 1: use Faulhaber sum recurrence.

Actually, let me re-derive the recurrence for G_j(N) = ∑_{k=0}^N m^k * k^j.

(1-m) * G_j(N) = 0^j + ∑_{r=0}^{j-1} C(j, r) (-1)^{j-r+1} * G_r(N) - m^{N+1} * N^j

So:
G_j(N) = (m^{N+1} * N^j - 0^j - ∑_{r=0}^{j-1} C(j, r) (-1)^{j-r+1} * G_r(N)) * inv(m-1) mod MOD

 "code": "MOD = 998244353\n\ndef compute_Gj_recurrence(N, m, max_j):\n    \"\"\"Compute G_j(N) = sum_{k=0}^N m^k * k^j for j=0..max_j using recurrence.\"\"\"\n    # Precompute binomial coefficients\n    C = [[0]*(max_j+2) for _ in range(max_j+2)]\n    for i in range(max_j+2):\n        C[i][0] = C[i][i] = 1\n        for j in range(1, i):\n            C[i][j] = (C[i-1][j-1] + C[i-1][j]) % MOD\n    \n    inv_m1 = mod_inv(m - 1) if m != 1 else None\n    \n    G = [0] * (max_j + 1)\n    \n    m_pow_N1 = pow(m, N+1, MOD)\n    \n    for j in range(max_j + 1):\n        if m == 1:\n            # Special case\n            if j == 0:\n                G[j] = (N + 1) % MOD\n            else:\n                # Use recurrence: (N+1)^{j+1} = sum_{r=0}^{j-1} C(j+1, r) G_r + (j+1) G_j\n                s = pow(N+1, j+1, MOD)\n                for r in range(j):\n                    s = (s - C[j+1][r] * G[r]) % MOD\n                G[j] = s * pow(j+1, MOD-2, MOD) % MOD\n        else:\n            if j == 0:\n                # G_0(N) = (m^{N+1} - 1) / (m - 1)\n                G[j] = (pow(m, N+1, MOD) - 1) * inv_m1 % MOD\n            else:\n                term = m_pow_N1 * pow(N, j, MOD) % MOD\n                if j == 0:\n                    term = (term - 1) % MOD\n                # Subtract sum_{r=0}^{j-1} C(j, r) (-1)^{j-r+1} G_r\n                sum_part = 0\n                for r in range(j):\n                    sign = -1 if (j - r + 1) % 2 == 0 else 1\n                    sum_part = (sum_part + sign * C[j][r] * G[r]) % MOD\n                G[j] = (term - sum_part) * inv_m1 % MOD\n    \n    return G\n\ndef mod_inv(a):\n    return pow(a, MOD-2, MOD)\n\n# Test against brute force\ndef brute_G(N, m, j):\n    total = 0\n    for k in range(N+1):\n        total = (total + pow(m, k, MOD) * pow(k, j, MOD)) % MOD\n    return total\n\nfor m in [1, 2, 3, 5]:\n    for N in [0, 1, 2, 3, 5, 10]:\n        G = compute_Gj_recurrence(N, m, 6)\n        for j in range(7):\n            b = brute_G(N, m, j)\n            if G[j] != b:\n                print(f\"Mismatch: m={m}, N={N}, j={j}, G={G[j]}, brute={b}\")\nprint(\"Done\")"
}

(-1)^{j+1} is +1 when j is odd, -1 when j is even.

Similarly for the sum term: (-1)^{j-r+1}:
- sign = 1 if (j-r+1) is even, i.e., if j-r is odd, i.e., if j and r have different parity.
- sign = -1 if (j-r+1) is odd, i.e., if j-r is even, i.e., if j and r have same parity.

So sign = 1 if (j-r) % 2 == 1 else -1.

In my code: `sign = -1 if (j - r + 1) % 2 == 0 else 1`:
- j-r+1 even → (j-r) odd → sign should be +1. My code gives -1. Wrong!

1) & rest\n    \n    A = [0] * (P+1)\n    for mask in range(1 << P):\n        for t in range(P+1):\n            A[t] = (A[t] + dp[mask][t]) % MOD\n    return A\n\ndef compute_G(N, m, max_j):\n    \"\"\"G_j(N) = sum_{k=0}^N m^k * k^j\"\"\"\n    G = [0] * (max_j + 1)\n    m_pow_N1 = pow(m, N+1, MOD)\n    inv_m1 = mod_inv(m - 1) if m != 1 else None\n    \n    for j in range(max_j + 1):\n        if m == 1:\n            if j == 0:\n                G[j] = (N + 1) % MOD\n            else:\n                s = pow(N+1, j+1, MOD)\n                for r in range(j):\n                    s = (s - C_binom[j+1][r] * G[r]) % MOD\n                G[j] = s * pow(j+1, MOD-2, MOD) % MOD\n        else:\n            if j == 0:\n                G[j] = (m_pow_N1 - 1) * inv_m1 % MOD\n            else:\n                term = m_pow_N1 * pow(N, j, MOD) % MOD\n                sign0 = 1 if j % 2 == 1 else -1\n                term = (term - sign0 * (G[0] - 1)) % MOD\n                for r in range(1, j):\n                    sign = 1 if (j - r) % 2 == 1 else -1\n                    term = (term - sign * C_binom[j][r] * G[r]) % MOD\n                G[j] = term * inv_m1 % MOD\n    return G\n\ndef sum_of_scores(n, m):\n    if m == 1:\n        # Only element is 1, score always 1, sum = n\n        return n % MOD\n    \n    primes, W = compute_primes_W(m)\n    P = len(primes)\n    A = compute_A(primes, W)\n    \n    inv_m = mod_inv(m)\n    \n    # C_j = sum_{t=j}^{P} A_t * m^{-t} * S1[t][j]\n    C = [0] * (P + 1)\n    for j in range(P + 1):\n        total = 0\n        for t in range(j, P + 1):\n            term = A[t] * pow(inv_m, t, MOD) % MOD * S1[t][j] % MOD\n            total = (total + term) % MOD\n        C[j] = total\n    \n    # Compute G_j(n) = sum_{k=0}^n m^k * k^j\n    G = compute_G(n, m, P)\n    \n    # H_j(n) = sum_{k=1}^n m^k * k^j = G_j(n) - (1 if j==0 else 0)\n    H = list(G)\n    H[0] = (H[0] - 1) % MOD  # subtract k=0 term: m^0 * 0^0 = 1\n\n    # Total = sum_j C_j * H_j\n    total = 0\n    for j in range(P + 1):\n        total = (total + C[j] * H[j]) % MOD\n    \n    return total\n\n# Test against brute force for small values\ndef brute(n, m):\n    def divisors(x):\n        cnt = 0\n        for i in range(1, int(x**0.5)+1):\n            if x % i == 0:\n                cnt += 1\n                if i != x // i:\n                    cnt += 1\n        return cnt\n    \n    MOD = 998244353\n    total = 0\n    for k in range(1, n+1):\n        def gen(seq):\n            nonlocal total\n            if len(seq) == k:\n                prod = 1\n                for x in seq:\n                    prod *= x\n                total = (total + divisors(prod)) % MOD\n                return\n            for v in range(1, m+1):\n                seq.append(v)\n                gen(seq)\n                seq.pop()\n        gen([])\n    return total\n\nprint(\"Testing against brute force...\")\nfor n in [1, 2, 3, 4]:\n    for m in [1, 2, 3, 4, 5, 6]:\n        b = brute(n, m)\n        f = sum_of_scores(n, m)\n        if b != f:\n            print(f\"FAIL: n={n}, m={m}, brute={b}, func={f}\")\n        else:\n            print(f\"OK: n={n}, m={m}, result={f}\")"
}

1) % MOD\n    total = 0\n    for j in range(P + 1):\n        total = (total + C[j] * H[j]) % MOD\n    return total\n\n# Verify S_k formula vs brute for k=1..6, m=13\ndef brute_Sk(k, m):\n    MOD = 998244353\n    def divisors(x):\n        cnt = 0\n        for i in range(1, int(x**0.5)+1):\n            if x % i == 0:\n                cnt += 1\n                if i != x // i:\n                    cnt += 1\n        return cnt\n    total = 0\n    def gen(seq):\n        nonlocal total\n        if len(seq) == k:\n            prod = 1\n            for x in seq:\n                prod *= x\n            total = (total + divisors(prod)) % MOD\n            return\n        for v in range(1, m+1):\n            seq.append(v)\n            gen(seq)\n            seq.pop()\n    gen([])\n    return total\n\nprimes, W = compute_primes_W(13)\nA = compute_A(primes, W)\nprint(\"A:\", A)\n\ninv_m = mod_inv(13)\nP = len(primes)\nC = [0] * (P + 1)\nfor j in range(P + 1):\n    total = 0\n    for t in range(j, P + 1):\n        term = A[t] * pow(inv_m, t, MOD) % MOD * S1[t][j] % MOD\n        total = (total + term) % MOD\n    C[j] = total\nprint(\"C:\", C)\n\nfor k in range(1, 7):\n    Sk = 0\n    mk = pow(13, k, MOD)\n    for j in range(P + 1):\n        Sk = (Sk + C[j] * pow(k, j, MOD)) % MOD\n    Sk = Sk * mk % MOD\n    brute = brute_Sk(k, 13)\n    print(f\"k={k}: formula={Sk}, brute={brute}, match={Sk==brute}\")\n\nEOF"

1) * inv_m1 % MOD\n        for t in range(1, max_t + 1):\n            term = m_pow_N1 * fall_n1[t] % MOD\n            term = (term - m * t % MOD * F[t - 1]) % MOD\n            F[t] = term * inv_m1 % MOD\n    return F\n\n\ndef sum_of_scores(n: int, m: int) -> int:\n    \"\"\" Calculate the sum of scores of all good sequences modulo 998244353.\n    \n    A good sequence is an integer sequence of length between 1 and n (inclusive),\n    where each element is between 1 and m (inclusive).\n    \n    The score of a good sequence is the number of positive divisors of X,\n    where X is the product of all elements in the sequence.\n    \n    The function computes the sum of scores of all possible good sequences.\n    There are sum(m^k for k in range(1, n+1)) such sequences in total.\n    \n    Args:\n        n: Maximum length of sequences (1 <= n <= 10^18)\n        m: Maximum value of elements (1 <= m <= 16)\n    \n    Returns:\n        The sum of all scores modulo 998244353\n    \n    >>> sum_of_scores(1, 7)\n    16\n    >>> sum_of_scores(3, 11)\n    16095\n    >>> sum_of_scores(81131, 14)\n    182955659\n    \"\"\"\n    if m == 1:\n        return n % MOD\n\n    primes, W = _compute_primes_W(m)\n    P = len(primes)\n    A = _compute_A(primes, W)\n\n    # F[t] = sum_{k=0}^n m^k * (k)_t\n    F = _compute_F(n, m, P)\n\n    inv_m = _mod_inv(m)\n\n    # total = A_0 * (F[0] - 1) + sum_{t=1}^P A_t * m^{-t} * F[t]\n    total = A[0] * (F[0] - 1) % MOD\n    inv_m_pow = 1  # m^0\n    for t in range(1, P + 1):\n        inv_m_pow = inv_m_pow * inv_m % MOD\n        total = (total + A[t] * inv_m_pow % MOD * F[t]) % MOD\n\n    return total"
